# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.

{
  "name": "find-local-parks",
  "private": true,
  "version": "0.1.0",
  "type": "module",
  "scripts": {
    "dev": "vite",
    "build": "vite build",
    "preview": "vite preview",
    "test": "vitest run"
  },
  "dependencies": {
    "leaflet": "^1.9.4",
    "react": "^19.3.1",
    "react-dom": "^19.3.1",
    "react-leaflet": "^5.0.0",
    "react-router": "^8.4.0"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "@tailwindcss/vite": "^4.3.3",
    "@testing-library/dom": "^10.4.2",
    "@testing-library/jest-dom": "^7.0.1",
    "@testing-library/react": "^16.3.3",
    "@testing-library/user-event": "^14.6.8",
    "@vitejs/plugin-react": "^6.1.2",
    "jsdom": "^29.1.1",
    "tailwindcss": "^4.3.3",
    "vite": "^8.3.4",
    "vitest": "^4.1.11"

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { Link, Route, Routes, useLocation, useMatch, useNavigate, useParams } from 'react-router'
import ParkDetails from './components/ParkDetails.jsx'
import ParkList from './components/ParkList.jsx'
import ParkMap from './components/ParkMap.jsx'
import ParkPreview from './components/ParkPreview.jsx'
import { useIsDesktop } from './data/useIsDesktop.js'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsRoute({ parks, onOpen }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  // Record the opened park so the list can return focus to its card.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (park) onOpen(park.id)
  }, [park, onOpen])

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <ParkDetails park={park} />
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry, lastParkId, setLastParkId }) {
  const clearLastParkId = useCallback(() => setLastParkId(null), [setLastParkId])

  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route
        path="/"
        element={
          <ParkList parks={parks} focusParkId={lastParkId} onFocusUsed={clearLastParkId} />
        }
      />
      <Route
        path="/parks/:id"
        element={<ParkDetailsRoute parks={parks} onOpen={setLastParkId} />}
      />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()
  // Id of the last-opened park. Lives in App state (not location.state) so
  // focus can return to its card after browser Back, which carries no state.
  const [lastParkId, setLastParkId] = useState(null)

  const navigate = useNavigate()
  const location = useLocation()
  const isDesktop = useIsDesktop()
  const selectedId = useMatch('/parks/:id')?.params.id ?? null
  // Phone-only marker preview; cleared on any navigation and when the viewport becomes desktop.
  const [previewId, setPreviewId] = useState(null)
  // Marker button to refocus after the preview closes.
  const [focusMarkerId, setFocusMarkerId] = useState(null)
  const clearFocusMarkerId = useCallback(() => setFocusMarkerId(null), [])

  useEffect(() => {
    setPreviewId(null)
  }, [location.key])

  useEffect(() => {
    if (isDesktop && previewId) {
      // Focus may be inside the preview: hand it back to the marker, as closing does.
      setFocusMarkerId(previewId)
      setPreviewId(null)
    }
  }, [isDesktop, previewId])

  const activePreviewId = isDesktop ? null : previewId
  const highlightedId = selectedId ?? activePreviewId
  const previewPark =
    status === 'ready' && activePreviewId
      ? (parks.find((park) => park.id === activePreviewId) ?? null)
      : null

  const activateMarker = useCallback(
    (id) => {
      if (isDesktop) {
        // Re-activating the park already open would push a duplicate history entry.
        if (id !== selectedId) navigate(`/parks/${id}`)
      } else setPreviewId(id)
    },
    [isDesktop, navigate, selectedId],
  )

  const closePreview = useCallback(() => {
    setFocusMarkerId(previewId)
    setPreviewId(null)
  }, [previewId])

  return (
    <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
      <header className="bg-forest px-5 py-4 text-white">
        <p className="text-xl font-extrabold">Find a Park</p>
        <p className="text-sm">New York City area</p>
        {status === 'ready' && <p className="text-sm">{parks.length} parks</p>}
      </header>
      <main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">
        <aside aria-label="Parks" className="p-5">
          <ParksPanel
            status={status}
            parks={parks}
            retry={retry}
            lastParkId={lastParkId}
            setLastParkId={setLastParkId}
          />
        </aside>
        <section aria-label="Map of parks" className="p-5">
          {status === 'ready' && (
            <div className="relative isolate h-[480px]">
              <ParkMap
                parks={parks}
                selectedId={highlightedId}
                isDesktop={isDesktop}
                onActivate={activateMarker}
                focusId={focusMarkerId}
                onFocused={clearFocusMarkerId}
              />
              {previewPark && (
                <ParkPreview
                  key={previewPark.id}
                  park={previewPark}
                  total={parks.length}
                  onClose={closePreview}
                />
              )}
            </div>
          )}
        </section>
      </main>
    </div>
  )
}
@import "tailwindcss";

/* Design tokens from design/README.md */
@theme {
  --color-paper: #F6F4EE;
  --color-surface: #FFFFFF;
  --color-line: #DAD6CB;
  --color-ink: #1B2620;
  --color-muted: #4F5B54;
  --color-forest: #1E5B3F;
  --color-forest-dark: #123826;
  --color-tag-bg: #E9EFE7;
  --color-tag-fg: #23472F;
  --color-amber: #B45309;
  --color-missing-bg: #F0EDE5;
  --color-missing-line: #C9C4B6;

  --font-sans: "Public Sans", "Segoe UI", system-ui, sans-serif;
}

/* Amber focus ring: apply `focus-ring` to every interactive element. */
@utility focus-ring {
  &:focus-visible {
    outline-style: solid;
    outline-width: 3px;
    outline-color: var(--color-amber);
    outline-offset: 2px;
  }
}

body {
  @apply bg-paper text-ink font-sans;
}
import { useEffect, useMemo, useRef } from 'react'
import L from 'leaflet'
import { MapContainer, Marker, TileLayer } from 'react-leaflet'
import 'leaflet/dist/leaflet.css'

// No-coordinates fallback: a fixed view over New York City.
const NYC_CENTER = [40.7128, -74.006]
const NYC_ZOOM = 11

const TILE_URL = 'https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png'
const TILE_ATTRIBUTION =
  '&copy; <a href="https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright">OpenStreetMap</a> contributors'

// Full literal class strings so Tailwind's scanner picks them up.
const BUTTON_CLASS =
  'focus-ring flex size-11 cursor-pointer items-center justify-center rounded-full border-[3px] border-white bg-forest text-base font-bold text-white shadow-md'
const SELECTED_BUTTON_CLASS =
  'focus-ring flex size-14 cursor-pointer items-center justify-center rounded-full border-[3px] border-white bg-amber text-lg font-bold text-white shadow-lg'
const SIZE = 44
const SELECTED_SIZE = 56

function escapeHtml(value) {
  return String(value ?? '')
    .replace(/&/g, '&amp;')
    .replace(/</g, '&lt;')
    .replace(/>/g, '&gt;')
    .replace(/"/g, '&quot;')
    .replace(/'/g, '&#39;')
}

function markerIcon(park, selected) {
  const size = selected ? SELECTED_SIZE : SIZE
  const name = escapeHtml(`${park.number}. ${park.name}`)
  return L.divIcon({
    className: 'park-marker',
    iconSize: [size, size],
    iconAnchor: [size / 2, size / 2],
    html:
      `<button type="button" data-park-id="${escapeHtml(park.id)}" class="${
        selected ? SELECTED_BUTTON_CLASS : BUTTON_CLASS
      }">` +
      `<span aria-hidden="true">${escapeHtml(park.number)}</span>` +
      `<span class="sr-only">${name}</span>` +
      `</button>`,
  })
}

/**
 * OpenStreetMap map of the parks that have coordinates. Each marker is a single focusable
 * `<button>` named "N. Park name"; the highlighted park's marker is amber and larger.
 * `isDesktop` is part of the props contract but the map renders the same at every width; the
 * caller decides what activation does. `focusId` asks the map to put focus on that park's marker button.
 */
export default function ParkMap({ parks, selectedId, onActivate, focusId, onFocused }) {
  const wrapperRef = useRef(null)
  const mapped = useMemo(() => parks.filter((park) => park.hasCoords), [parks])

  // Stable icons per park and state, so unrelated renders never replace a marker's DOM.
  const icons = useMemo(() => {
    const cache = new Map()
    for (const park of mapped) {
      cache.set(park.id, { normal: markerIcon(park, false), selected: markerIcon(park, true) })
    }
    return cache
  }, [mapped])

  const bounds = useMemo(
    () => (mapped.length > 0 ? L.latLngBounds(mapped.map((p) => [p.lat, p.lng])) : null),
    [mapped],
  )

  // Runs after the markers' own effects, so it targets the current icon element.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!focusId) return
    const button = wrapperRef.current?.querySelector(
      `button[data-park-id="${CSS.escape(focusId)}"]`,
    )
    button?.focus()
    onFocused?.()
  }, [focusId, selectedId, onFocused])

  const viewProps = bounds
    ? { bounds, boundsOptions: { padding: [32, 32], maxZoom: 15 } }
    : { center: NYC_CENTER, zoom: NYC_ZOOM }

  return (
    <div ref={wrapperRef} className="h-full w-full">
      <MapContainer {...viewProps} className="h-full w-full rounded-[10px]">
        <TileLayer url={TILE_URL} attribution={TILE_ATTRIBUTION} />
        {mapped.map((park) => {
          const selected = park.id === selectedId
          const icon = icons.get(park.id)
          return (
            <Marker
              key={park.id}
              position={[park.lat, park.lng]}
              icon={selected ? icon.selected : icon.normal}
              keyboard={false}
              zIndexOffset={selected ? 1000 : 0}
              eventHandlers={{ click: () => onActivate?.(park.id) }}
            />
          )
        })}
      </MapContainer>
    </div>
  )
}

29	          {park.address ? (
30	            <span className="block text-muted">{park.address}</span>
31	          ) : (
32	            <span className="block text-muted">Address not listed</span>
33	          )}{' '}
34	          {tags.length > 0 && (
35	            <span data-testid="park-tags" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
36	              {tags.map((label, index) => (
37	                <Fragment key={`${index}-${label}`}>
38	                  <span className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
39	                    {label}
40	                  </span>{' '}
41	                </Fragment>
42	              ))}
43	            </span>
44	          )}
45	        </span>
46	        <svg
47	          aria-hidden="true"
48	          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
49	          className="size-5 shrink-0 self-center text-muted"
50	          fill="none"
51	          stroke="currentColor"
52	          strokeWidth="2"
53	        >
54	          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
55	        </svg>
56	      </Link>
57	    </li>
58	  )
59	}
60	
61	/**
62	 * The "All parks" list. Parks are rendered in loader order with loader numbers.
63	 * If `focusParkId` matches a card on mount, that card's link gets focus and
64	 * `onFocusUsed` is called so a later fresh visit does not steal focus.
65	 */
66	export default function ParkList({ parks, focusParkId, onFocusUsed }) {
67	  const linkRefs = useRef(new Map())
68	
69	  useEffect(() => {
70	    if (!focusParkId) return
71	    linkRefs.current.get(focusParkId)?.focus()
72	    onFocusUsed?.()
73	    // Run once on mount: the id is read once, then cleared by the parent.
74	  }, [])
75	
76	  return (
77	    <div>
78	      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>
79	      <p className="mt-1 text-muted">Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions.</p>
80	      <ul className="mt-4 flex flex-col gap-3">
81	        {parks.map((park) => (
82	          <ParkCard
83	            key={park.id}
84	            park={park}
85	            linkRef={(node) => {
86	              if (node) linkRefs.current.set(park.id, node)
87	              else linkRefs.current.delete(park.id)
88	            }}
89	          />
90	        ))}
91	      </ul>
92	    </div>
93	  )
94	}
95	import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react'
96	import { Link } from 'react-router'
97	import { amenityLabel, display, formatAcres, formatRating, osmUrl } from '../data/format.js'

99	// Fallback copy, word for word from design/README.md.
100	const NO_ADDRESS = 'Address not listed'
101	const NO_DESCRIPTION = 'No description has been provided for this park.'
102	const NO_RATING = 'Not yet rated'
103	const NO_PHOTOS = 'No photos for this park yet'
104	const NOT_LISTED = 'Not listed'
105	const NO_AMENITIES = 'No amenities listed'

107	function NoPhoto() {
108	  return (
109	    <div
110	      className="flex aspect-[16/9] flex-col items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-[10px] border-2 border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg p-4 text-center text-muted"
111	    >
112	      <svg
113	        aria-hidden="true"
114	        viewBox="0 0 24 24"
115	        className="size-7"
116	        fill="none"
117	        stroke="currentColor"
118	        strokeWidth="1.75"
119	        strokeLinecap="round"
120	        strokeLinejoin="round"
121	      >
122	        <rect x="3" y="4" width="18" height="16" rx="2" />
123	        <path d="m3 16 5-5 4 4 3-3 6 6" />
124	        <circle cx="15.5" cy="8.5" r="1.5" />
125	      </svg>
126	      <p>{NO_PHOTOS}</p>
127	    </div>
128	  )
129	}

131	/** First photo only, with a "Photo 1 of N" label. A load failure swaps in the no-photo box. */
132	function PhotoArea({ park }) {
133	  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false)
134	  const src = park.images[0]
135	
136	  if (!src || failed) return <NoPhoto />
137	
138	  return (
139	    <figure className="relative overflow-hidden rounded-[10px] bg-missing-bg">
140	      <img
141	        src={src}
142	        alt={`Photo of ${park.name}`}
143	        onError={() => setFailed(true)}
144	        className="block aspect-[16/9] w-full object-cover"
145	      />
146	      <figcaption className="absolute right-2 bottom-2 rounded-full bg-ink/85 px-2.5 py-0.5 text-[13px] font-semibold text-white">
147	        Photo 1 of {park.images.length}
148	      </figcaption>
149	    </figure>
150	  )
151	}
152	
153	function FactTile({ label, value }) {
154	  const { missing, text } = value
155	  return (
156	    <div
157	      className={`min-w-0 break-words rounded-[10px] border p-3 ${
158	        missing ? 'border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg' : 'border-line bg-surface'
159	      }`}
160	    >
161	      <dt className="text-sm text-muted">{label}</dt>
162	      <dd className={missing ? 'italic text-muted' : 'font-semibold'}>{text}</dd>
163	    </div>
164	  )
165	}

167	/** Full details for one normalized park. Every missing value shows its labelled fallback. */
168	export default function ParkDetails({ park }) {
169	  const headingRef = useRef(null)
170	
171	  // Opening details (or moving to another park) puts focus on the heading.
172	  useEffect(() => {
173	    headingRef.current?.focus()
174	  }, [park.id])
175	
176	  const address = display(park.address, NO_ADDRESS)
177	  const about = display(park.description, NO_DESCRIPTION)
178	
179	  return (
180	    <article>
181	      <Link
182	        to="/"
183	        className="focus-ring inline-flex min-h-11 min-w-11 items-center gap-1 rounded-lg border border-line bg-surface px-3 font-semibold text-forest hover:border-forest"
184	      >
185	        <svg
186	          aria-hidden="true"
187	          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
188	          className="size-5"
189	          fill="none"
190	          stroke="currentColor"
191	          strokeWidth="2"
192	        >
193	          <path d="m15 6-6 6 6 6" />
194	        </svg>
195	        All parks
196	      </Link>
197	
198	      <div className="mt-4">
199	        <PhotoArea key={park.id} park={park} />
200	      </div>
201	
202	      <h1
203	        ref={headingRef}
204	        tabIndex={-1}
205	        className="mt-4 text-[28px] leading-tight font-extrabold"
206	      >
207	        {park.name}
208	      </h1>
209	      <p className="mt-1 text-muted">{address.text}</p>
210	
211	      {park.hasCoords && (
212	        <p className="mt-2">
213	          <a
214	            href={osmUrl(park.lat, park.lng)}
215	            target="_blank"
216	            rel="noopener noreferrer"
217	            className="focus-ring inline-flex min-h-11 items-center rounded font-semibold text-forest underline"
218	          >
219	            Open in OpenStreetMap{' '}
220	            <span className="sr-only">(opens in new tab)</span>
221	          </a>
222	        </p>
223	      )}
224	
225	      <dl className="mt-3 grid grid-cols-3 gap-2">
226	        <FactTile label="Hours" value={display(park.hours, NOT_LISTED)} />
227	        <FactTile label="Size" value={display(formatAcres(park.acreage), NOT_LISTED)} />
228	        <FactTile label="Rating" value={display(formatRating(park.rating), NO_RATING)} />
229	      </dl>
230	
231	      <section aria-labelledby="park-about" className="mt-5">
232	        <h2 id="park-about" className="text-lg font-bold">
233	          About
234	        </h2>
235	        <p className={`mt-1 ${about.missing ? 'italic text-muted' : ''}`}>{about.text}</p>
236	      </section>
237	
238	      <section aria-labelledby="park-amenities" className="mt-5">
239	        <h2 id="park-amenities" className="text-lg font-bold">
240	          Amenities
241	        </h2>
242	        {park.amenities.length > 0 ? (
243	          <ul aria-label="Amenities" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
244	            {park.amenities.map((token, index) => (
245	              <li
246	                key={`${index}-${token}`}
247	                className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-3 py-1 text-tag-fg"
248	              >
249	                <svg
250	                  aria-hidden="true"
251	                  viewBox="0 0 24 24"
252	                  className="size-4"
253	                  fill="none"
254	                  stroke="currentColor"
255	                  strokeWidth="2.5"
256	                >
257	                  <path d="m5 12 5 5 9-10" />
258	                </svg>
259	                {amenityLabel(token)}
260	              </li>
261	            ))}
262	          </ul>
263	        ) : (
264	          <p className="mt-1 italic text-muted">{NO_AMENITIES}</p>
265	        )}
266	      </section>
267	    </article>
268	  )
269	}

277	/** Phone-only preview card for a tapped marker. Focus moves to the card when it opens. */
278	export default function ParkPreview({ park, total, onClose }) {
279	  const cardRef = useRef(null)
280	
281	  useEffect(() => {
282	    cardRef.current?.focus()
283	  }, [park.id])
284	
285	  const address = display(park.address, NO_ADDRESS)
286	  const hours = display(park.hours, NO_HOURS)
287	  const headingId = `park-preview-${park.id}`
288

290	    <section
291	      ref={cardRef}
292	      tabIndex={-1}

296	      }}
297	      className="focus-ring absolute inset-x-3 bottom-8 z-[1001] rounded-2xl bg-surface p-4 shadow-lg"
298	    >
299	      <div className="flex items-start gap-3">
300	        <div className="min-w-0 flex-1">
301	          <p className="text-sm font-bold text-amber">

303	          </p>
304	          <h2 id={headingId} className="text-xl font-extrabold">
305	            {park.name}
306	          </h2>
307	          <p className="text-muted">{address.text}</p>
308	          <p className="text-muted">
309	            {hours.missing ? (
310	              hours.text
311	            ) : (
312	              <>
313	                <span className="sr-only">Hours: </span>
314	                {hours.text}
315	              </>
316	            )}
317	          </p>
318	        </div>
319	        <button
320	          type="button"
321	          aria-label="Close park preview"
322	          onClick={onClose}
323	          className="focus-ring flex size-11 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-missing-bg text-ink"
324	        >
325	          <svg
326	            aria-hidden="true"
327	            viewBox="0 0 24 24"
328	            className="size-5"
329	            fill="none"
330	            stroke="currentColor"
331	            strokeWidth="2"
332	          >
333	            <path d="M6 6l12 12M18 6 6 18" />
334	          </svg>
335	        </button>
336	      </div>
337	      <Link
338	        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}
339	        className="focus-ring mt-3 flex min-h-11 w-full items-center justify-center gap-1 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-bold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
340	      >
341	        View park details
342	        <svg
343	          aria-hidden="true"
344	          viewBox="0 0 24 24"

346	          fill="none"
347	          stroke="currentColor"
348	          strokeWidth="2"
349	        >
350	          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
351	        </svg>
352	      </Link>
353	    </section>
354	  )
355	}
356	import { useEffect, useState } from 'react'
357	
358	const QUERY = '(min-width: 768px)'
359	
360	function getMatch() {
361	  if (typeof window === 'undefined' || typeof window.matchMedia !== 'function') return true
362	  return window.matchMedia(QUERY).matches
363	}
364	
365	/** True at viewport widths ≥ 768px. Without `matchMedia` (e.g. jsdom), assumes desktop. */
366	export function useIsDesktop() {
367	  const [isDesktop, setIsDesktop] = useState(getMatch)
368	
369	  useEffect(() => {
370	    if (typeof window === 'undefined' || typeof window.matchMedia !== 'function') return undefined
371	    const mql = window.matchMedia(QUERY)
372	    const onChange = () => setIsDesktop(mql.matches)
373	    onChange()
374	    mql.addEventListener('change', onChange)
375	    return () => mql.removeEventListener('change', onChange)
376	  }, [])
377	
378	  return isDesktop
379	}

521	async function findCards() {

523	  return within(list).getAllByRole('listitem')
524	}

527	  return screen

529	    .find((li) => within(li).queryByText(name, { exact: true }) !== null)
530	}

669	    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
670	    const user = userEvent.setup()
671	
672	    function TestNav() {
673	      const navigate = useNavigate()
674	      return (
675	        <>
676	          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate(-1)}>
677	            back
678	          </button>
679	          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate('/parks/nope')}>
680	            elsewhere
681	          </button>
682	          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate('/')}>
683	            home
684	          </button>
685	        </>
686	      )
687	    }
688	
689	    render(
690	      <MemoryRouter initialEntries={['/']}>
691	        <TestNav />
692	        <App />
693	      </MemoryRouter>,
694	    )
695	    await findCards()
696	    await user.click(within(cardFor('Highland Dog Park')).getByRole('link'))
697	    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Highland Dog Park' })).toBeInTheDocument()
698	
699	    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'back' }))
700	    await findCards()
701	    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(within(cardFor('Highland Dog Park')).getByRole('link'))
702	
703	    // The id was consumed: a later fresh visit to / does not steal focus.
704	    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'elsewhere' }))
705	    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Park not found' })).toBeInTheDocument()
706	    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'home' }))
707	    await findCards()
708	    expect(screen.getAllByRole('listitem').map((li) => within(li).getByRole('link'))).not.toContain(
709	      document.activeElement,
710	    )
711	  })
712	})

714	function stubData(mutate) {

716	  mutate?.(data)
717	  vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ ok: true, json: async () => data }))

720	async function openDetails(id, mutate) {
721	  stubData(mutate)
722	  renderAt(`/parks/${id}`)
723	  return screen.findByRole('heading', { level: 1 })
724	}
725	
726	function tile(label) {
727	  const dt = screen.getByText(label, { selector: 'dt' })
728	  return { box: dt.parentElement, value: dt.nextElementSibling }
729	}
730	
731	function expectMissingTile(label, text) {
732	  const { box, value } = tile(label)
733	  expect(value).toHaveTextContent(text)
734	  expect(value).toHaveClass('italic', 'text-muted')
735	  expect(box).toHaveClass('border-dashed', 'border-missing-line', 'bg-missing-bg')
736	}
737	
738	function amenityLabels() {
739	  const list = screen.getByRole('list', { name: 'Amenities' })
740	  return within(list)
741	    .getAllByRole('listitem')
742	    .map((li) => li.textContent)
743	}

747	    const heading = await openDetails('prospect-park')
748	    expect(heading).toHaveTextContent('Prospect Park')
749	
750	    const back = screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'All parks' })
751	    const img = screen.getByRole('img', { name: 'Photo of Prospect Park' })
752	    const photoLabel = screen.getByText('Photo 1 of 2')
753	    const address = screen.getByText('Brooklyn, NY 11225')
754	    const osm = screen.getByRole('link', { name: /^Open in OpenStreetMap/ })
755	    const facts = screen.getByText('Hours', { selector: 'dt' }).closest('dl')
756	    const about = screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 2, name: 'About' })
757	    const amenities = screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 2, name: 'Amenities' })
758	
759	    const order = [back, img, photoLabel, heading, address, osm, facts, about, amenities]
760	    for (let i = 1; i < order.length; i++) {
761	      expect(
762	        order[i - 1].compareDocumentPosition(order[i]) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING,
763	      ).toBeTruthy()
764	    }

769	      'https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat=40.6602&mlon=-73.969#map=16/40.6602/-73.969',
770	    )
771	    expect(tile('Hours').value).toHaveTextContent('6:00 AM - 1:00 AM')
772	    expect(tile('Size').value).toHaveTextContent('526 acres')
773	    expect(tile('Rating').value).toHaveTextContent('4.7 / 5')
774	    for (const label of ['Hours', 'Size', 'Rating']) {

776	      expect(tile(label).value).not.toHaveClass('italic')
777	    }

787	    ])

789	    items.forEach((li) => expect(li.querySelector('svg')).not.toBeNull())
790	  })

932	// jsdom has no matchMedia: stub it per test. `set` simulates a viewport change.
933	function stubViewport(desktop) {
934	  const listeners = new Set()
935	  const mql = {
936	    matches: desktop,
937	    media: '(min-width: 768px)',
938	    addEventListener: (_type, fn) => listeners.add(fn),
939	    removeEventListener: (_type, fn) => listeners.delete(fn),
940	  }
941	  window.matchMedia = vi.fn(() => mql)
942	  return {
943	    set(value) {
944	      mql.matches = value
945	      act(() => listeners.forEach((fn) => fn()))
946	    },
947	  }
948	}
949	
950	afterEach(() => {
951	  delete window.matchMedia
952	})
953	
954	function LocationDisplay() {
955	  const location = useLocation()
956	  return <p data-testid="location">{location.pathname}</p>
957	}
958	
959	function renderWithLocation(path) {
960	  return render(
961	    <MemoryRouter initialEntries={[path]}>
962	      <LocationDisplay />
963	      <App />
964	    </MemoryRouter>,
965	  )
966	}

968	function mapRegion() {
969	  return screen.getByRole('region', { name: 'Map of parks' })
970	}

972	const MARKER_NAME = /^\d+\. /
973	
974	function markerNamed(name) {
975	  return within(mapRegion()).getByRole('button', { name: new RegExp(`^\\d+\\. ${name}$`) })
976	}
977	
978	function findMarkerByName(name) {
979	  return within(mapRegion()).findByRole('button', { name: new RegExp(`^\\d+\\. ${name}$`) })
980	}

1257	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
1258	import { resolve } from 'node:path'
1259	import { render, screen } from '@testing-library/react'
1260	import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event'
1261	import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest'
1262	import { normalizeParks } from '../data/parks.js'
1263	import ParkMap from './ParkMap.jsx'
1264	
1265	const sample = JSON.parse(
1266	  readFileSync(resolve(process.cwd(), 'public/data/parks.sample.json'), 'utf8'),
1267	)
1268	
1269	function parksFrom(mutate) {
1270	  const data = structuredClone(sample)
1271	  mutate?.(data)
1272	  return normalizeParks(data)
1273	}
1274	
1275	function markerButtons() {
1276	  return screen.queryAllByRole('button', { name: /^\d+\. / })
1277	}
1278	
1279	function markerFor(name) {
1280	  return screen.getByRole('button', { name: new RegExp(`^\\d+\\. ${name}$`) })
1281	}
1282	
1283	afterEach(() => vi.unstubAllGlobals())
1284	
1285	describe('ParkMap', () => {
1286	  it('renders one numbered marker button per park, named "N. Park name", none amber', () => {
1287	    const parks = parksFrom()
1288	    render(<ParkMap parks={parks} selectedId={null} onActivate={() => {}} />)
1289	    const buttons = markerButtons()
1290	    expect(buttons).toHaveLength(12)
1291	    parks.forEach((park, i) => {
1292	      expect(buttons[i]).toHaveAccessibleName(`${park.number}. ${park.name}`)
1293	      expect(buttons[i].tagName).toBe('BUTTON')
1294	      expect(buttons[i]).toHaveAttribute('type', 'button')
1295	      expect(buttons[i]).toHaveClass('focus-ring', 'bg-forest', 'size-11')
1296	      expect(buttons[i]).not.toHaveClass('bg-amber')
1297	    })
1298	  })
1299	
1300	  it('uses divIcons only: no default image icon, no default div-icon box', () => {
1301	    const { container } = render(
1302	      <ParkMap parks={parksFrom()} selectedId={null} onActivate={() => {}} />,
1303	    )
1304	    expect(container.querySelector('.leaflet-marker-icon img')).toBeNull()
1305	    expect(container.querySelector('.leaflet-div-icon')).toBeNull()
1306	    expect(container.querySelectorAll('.leaflet-marker-icon.park-marker')).toHaveLength(12)
1307	  })
1308	
1309	  it('makes the selected park amber and larger, and keeps the rest forest', () => {
1310	    render(<ParkMap parks={parksFrom()} selectedId="prospect-park" onActivate={() => {}} />)
1311	    const selected = markerFor('Prospect Park')
1312	    expect(selected).toHaveClass('bg-amber', 'size-14')
1313	    expect(selected).not.toHaveClass('bg-forest')
1314	    for (const button of markerButtons().filter((b) => b !== selected)) {
1315	      expect(button).toHaveClass('bg-forest', 'size-11')
1316	      expect(button).not.toHaveClass('bg-amber')
1317	    }
1318	    // Larger icon box and raised above the others.
1319	    const icon = selected.closest('.leaflet-marker-icon')
1320	    expect(icon.style.width).toBe('56px')
1321	    expect(Number(icon.style.zIndex)).toBeGreaterThan(
1322	      Number(markerFor('Riverside Commons').closest('.leaflet-marker-icon').style.zIndex),
1323	    )
1324	  })
1325	
1326	  it('gives a park without coordinates no marker', () => {

1329	    })
1330	    render(<ParkMap parks={parks} selectedId={null} onActivate={() => {}} />)

1349	    )
1350	    expect(screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'OpenStreetMap' })).toHaveAttribute(

1359	  })
1360	
1361	  it('gives each marker button exactly one Tab stop, in order', async () => {
1362	    const parks = parksFrom()
1363	    const user = userEvent.setup()
1364	    render(<ParkMap parks={parks} selectedId={null} onActivate={() => {}} />)
1365	    const buttons = markerButtons()
1366	    const reached = []
1367	    for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) {
1368	      await user.tab()
1369	      if (buttons.includes(document.activeElement)) reached.push(document.activeElement)
1370	    }
1371	    // Tab wraps around the document, so cut at the first repeat.
1372	    const firstPass = reached.slice(0, buttons.length)
1373	    expect(firstPass).toEqual(buttons)
1374	    // The Leaflet marker wrapper itself is never a Tab stop.
1375	    for (const button of buttons) {
1376	      expect(button.closest('.leaflet-marker-icon')).not.toHaveAttribute('tabindex')
1377	    }
1378	  })
1379	
1380	  it('calls onActivate with the park id on click and on Enter', async () => {
1381	    const onActivate = vi.fn()
1382	    const user = userEvent.setup()
1383	    render(<ParkMap parks={parksFrom()} selectedId={null} onActivate={onActivate} />)
1384	    await user.click(markerFor('Prospect Park'))
1385	    expect(onActivate).toHaveBeenLastCalledWith('prospect-park')
1386	
1387	    markerFor('Highland Dog Park').focus()

1389	    expect(onActivate).toHaveBeenLastCalledWith('highland-dog-park')
1390	    expect(onActivate).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2)
1391	  })
1392

1398	    }
1399	    vi.stubGlobal('navigator', { ...navigator, geolocation })
1400	    render(<ParkMap parks={parksFrom()} selectedId="prospect-park" onActivate={() => {}} />)
1401	    expect(geolocation.getCurrentPosition).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
1402	    expect(geolocation.watchPosition).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
1403	  })

  **Existing tests I had to change beyond duplicate link names** (the acceptance criteria only expected selector changes for those)
  1. The new loading `<h1>` "All parks" made some `findByRole('heading')` waits resolve before the data loaded. Those tests now wait for the list or `article` instead.
  2. The toggle comes before the cards in tab order, and jsdom doesn't apply CSS, so the two tab-order tests now start focus at the Map button.
  3. "All parks" link lookups are now scoped `within(aside)`.
  4. The "duplicate history entry" test already logged an unhandled `Invalid LatLng (NaN, NaN)` error, which made `npm test` exit 1. Two marker clicks within Leaflet's 200ms synthetic double-click window zoom a map jsdom can't measure. I added a 250ms wait between the clicks, and the suite now exits 0.
  
  **Real-browser check** (headless Chrome driven over the DevTools protocol against `vite preview`)
  - **Horizontal scroll and headings:** `scrollWidth` equals `innerWidth` on the list, details and Map tab at 375px, and on the list and details at 1280px. Each view has exactly one `<h1>`.
  - **Keyboard flow:** list → details → back by keyboard works at both widths. Every stop has the 3px amber ring: toggle buttons (44px tall), cards, the details `<h1>`, back links, the zoom-in button (44×44) and markers. Focus returns to the card.
  - **Map from the phone:** the Map tab fills 375×661 with tiles loaded. Marker → preview → details → header "All parks" lands back on the Map tab with `aria-pressed="true"` and focus on marker 8.
  - **Desktop sidebar:** the aside is 400px and scrolls by itself; the page itself doesn't scroll.
  - **Visual comparison:** I compared screenshots with design screens 01/02/04/06 and they match closely.
  
  **Incomplete or risky**
  - I did not look at the 05 (phone map selected) screenshot or test touch input.
  - The attribution links have the focus ring but are smaller than 44px. They are inline text links, which the WCAG target-size rule exempts.
  - The aside keeps `p-4` under the toggle as the spec says, so the space above "All parks" on phone is a bit larger than in the design.
  - `npm install` was needed first, because `node_modules` was missing `leaflet`.

+/**
+ * Keeps Leaflet's size in step with its container. Leaflet measures once, so a container that
+ * was `display:none` (phone List tab) or changed size (resize, tab switch) shows grey tiles until
+ * `invalidateSize()` runs. Zero-size observations (pane hidden) are skipped so the view survives
+ * hide/show. If the map was created while hidden, the initial view is applied once it first gets a size.
+ */
+function ResizeToContainer({ fitInitialView }) {
+  const map = useMap()
+
+  useEffect(() => {
+    if (typeof ResizeObserver === 'undefined') return undefined
+    const container = map.getContainer()
+    const start = map.getSize()
+    let everSized = start.x > 0 && start.y > 0
+    const observer = new ResizeObserver((entries) => {
+      const rect = entries[entries.length - 1]?.contentRect
+      if (!rect || rect.width === 0 || rect.height === 0) return
+      map.invalidateSize()
+      if (!everSized) {
+        everSized = true
+        fitInitialView(map)
+      }
+    })
+    observer.observe(container)
+    return () => observer.disconnect()
+  }, [map, fitInitialView])
+
+  return null
+}
+

64	### Classify
65	
66	1. Once every layer has reported — and not before — render a verdict on each finding, ahead of any deduplication or grouping. Disregard any severity a reviewing subagent assigned — they lack the context to grade.
67	
68	   If `## Review Triage Log` already has rows — a loopback or a resumed review — check each finding against them first. Same location and same claim as a logged row, and the code there still reads as the row describes: keep the row's verdict and route, write the row again with `carried` in front of the evidence, skip verification, and never patch or defer it again. Verify everything else as below.
69	
70	   For each finding:
71	   - A gap finding from the verification-gap layer arrives pre-verified — that layer's evidence rules made it read the tests and run the searches it cites, and triage trusts the claim as filed. Skip verification, render the verdict from the filed evidence, and weigh its filed disposition when routing. Its `Other findings` are verified like everything else.
72	   - **Verify the finding's claim.** At the cited file and line, does the bad outcome the reviewer describes actually occur? Read beyond the changed lines — follow callers, guards upstream, etc — until you can answer yes or no. A different finding about nearby code does not settle this one. Judge whether the problem is real, not whether the proposed fix is plausible. Code that loudly fails on a situation you never showed the program can reach is correct behavior, not a defect.
73	   - **Render exactly one verdict** from what verification established — the verdict is the whole triage decision; there is no separate keep-or-dismiss.
74	     - `high` (intolerable), `medium` (tolerable), `low` (cosmetic or negligible) — the bad outcome is real. Assign severity by how much it hurts end users or developers. For developer-only problems (inconsistent design, eroded invariants, duplicated sources of truth), name where it will cause trouble — which caller will diverge, which rule will break. A vague "this is messy" with no named harm is not a severity grade; use `false` or `maybe-false` instead. When the harm is real but you cannot tell how bad, pick the higher grade.
75	     - `false` — you checked, and the bad outcome does not happen at the cited location. Write what disproves this specific claim. A true fact about nearby code that does not disprove the claim does not count.
76	     - `maybe-false` — you could not tell whether the bad outcome happens. Write what you would need to check to find out. Use this only when the diff and surrounding code leave the question open; when they are enough to decide, pick `high`, `medium`, `low`, or `false`.
77	
78	   - Every finding gets one row in the `## Review Triage Log` section of `{spec_file}` — verdict plus its evidence in a sentence or two; never drop, merge, or silently skip one.
79	
80	   Reject `false` findings on their refutation.
81	
82	   Reject `low` findings when it is unlikely that users or developers would meet the defect in everyday use (judged plainly — no proof needed) and the fix is more than a direct correction or deletion — adding guards, branches, parameters, or other complexity.
83	
84	   Out of scope: reject or defer a finding as out of scope only when the intent itself excludes it — not because the spec's scope section, the plan, or the shape of the diff says so. If only those would exclude it, keep the finding: the spec or plan drew the line somewhere the intent did not, so it routes to intent_gap or bad_spec, never to patch or defer.
85	
86	   Reject any finding whose fix is to edit this build's spec.
87	
88	   All remaining findings continue to grouping.
89	
90	2. Group the survivors by shared root cause — two findings belong in one entry only when the same defect produced both. Same location alone is not a shared root cause, and neither is a shared fix. An entry carries every member's verified bad outcome and the highest verdict among them (`high` > `medium` > `low` > `maybe-false`).
91	3. Route each entry into exactly one triage category. A group that includes verified `high`, `medium`, or `low` members routes by its highest such verdict — not to defer just because a member is `maybe-false`. The first three are **this story's problem** — caused or exposed by the current change. The last is **not this story's problem**.
92	   - **intent_gap** — caused by the change; cannot be resolved from the spec because the captured intent is incomplete. Do not infer intent unless there is exactly one possible reading.
93	   - **bad_spec** — caused by the change, including direct deviations from spec. The spec should have been clear enough to prevent it. When in doubt between bad_spec and patch, prefer bad_spec — a spec-level fix is more likely to produce coherent code.
94	   - **patch** — caused by the change; its smallest fix is trivial, adds no public surface, and guards no state you did not demonstrate. Just part of the diff. A finding whose smallest fix fails any of those conditions routes to intent_gap when the spec does not settle that fix, otherwise to bad_spec.
95	   - **defer** — pre-existing issue not caused by this story; or an entry whose members are all `maybe-false` and the claim, if true, would be `medium` or `high` — record that severity marked unverified, plus what would settle it (if it would only be `low`, reject it with the same note); or any entry whose fix edits agent-context files (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, rules, etc).
96

If neither exists, process patch and defer normally. Before each loopback, read `{spec_file}` frontmatter `review_loop_iteration` (missing means `0`), increment it by 1, and write it back. If it exceeds 5, HALT and escalate to the human.

Revert code changes. Read the `## Spec Change Log` in `{spec_file}` and strictly respect all logged constraints when amending the non-frozen sections that contain the root cause. 100	   - **patch** — Auto-fix. These are the only findings that survive loopbacks. Re-engage the step-03 implementation subagent — the same one, addressed by the name or id its launch returned; a fresh launch is not re-engagement. Send it one message, exactly this, with the findings filled in:

1	# Edge Case Hunter Review
2	
3	**Goal:** You are a pure path tracer. Never comment on whether code is good or bad; only list missing handling.
4	When a diff is provided, scan only the diff hunks and list boundaries that are directly reachable from the changed lines and lack an explicit guard in the diff.
5	When no diff is provided (full file or function), treat the entire provided content as the scope.
6	Ignore the rest of the codebase unless the provided content explicitly references external functions.
7	A brief secondary deletion check runs as Step 4 when the diff removes code.
8	A claims check runs as Step 5.
9	
10	**Inputs:**
11	- **content** — Content to review, or a path to read it from: diff, full file, or function
12	- **also_consider** (optional) — Areas to keep in mind during review alongside normal edge-case analysis

21

33	- Walk all branching paths: control flow (conditionals, loops, error handlers, early returns) and domain boundaries (where values, states, or conditions transition). Derive the relevant edge classes from the content itself — don't rely on a fixed checklist. Examples: missing else/default, unguarded inputs, off-by-one loops, arithmetic overflow, implicit type coercion, race conditions, timeout gaps
34	- Consider implicit branches: the diff special-cases or changes the handling of one or more members of a fixed set of values — enums, status codes, sentinels, type tags, flags, value ranges. The rest of the set is implicit branches (e.g. the diff changes the `RED` and `YELLOW` cases of a `RED`/`YELLOW`/`GREEN` enum; `GREEN` is the implicit branch)
35	- Consider handle lifetime: when the changed code re-checks, re-fetches, or re-validates something it already held — a handle, index, id, pointer — the re-check exists because an intervening call can invalidate it. Identify that call, what it does to the thing held, and what the changed code silently skips when the re-check fails
36	- For each call site the diff adds or changes — in test files as well as production code — read the callee's declaration and check the call against it: argument count, order, types, and defaults. Report any mismatch
37	- For each path: determine whether the content handles it
38	- Collect only the unhandled paths as findings — discard handled ones silently

[tool result for toolu_01J5VgAfMAxP8x5QeP1pqvrd]
1	# Verification Gap Review
2	
3	**Goal:** Find changed behavior that could break without reliable verification catching it. Ask one question — "if the behavior this change is supposed to produce broke where it's actually used, would verification fail?" Do not hunt for correctness bugs, but report genuine problems you notice while tracing verification.
4	
5	The main verification gap shapes are:
6	
7	1. **Regression gap:** the changed code regresses where it's used, and no test covering that use would fail.
8	2. **Missing-adoption gap:** a place that should now use the new behavior doesn't; it handles the same case its own way, or not at all, and no test would flag the omission.
9	3. **Broken-verification gap:** a test appears to cover the changed behavior, but would not actually protect it because it is skipped, flaky, not run in the normal verification path, or too weak to observe the regression.
10	
11	## Evidence Rules
12	
13	- Read a test before claiming what it covers, runs, asserts, or misses.
14	- Before claiming no test exists, search the whole repo by the symbol under test and by import references; expected file locations are not enough.
15	- Never assert what you did not verify. If a finding cannot be grounded, drop it.
16	- In a finding, say what you actually checked — "none of the tests I read cover this" — and show how far you looked. Say a test doesn't exist anywhere only when the symbol/import-reference search actually shows that.
17	- Do not assign severity, confidence, priority, or ranking.
18	
19	## Review Sequence
20	
21	### Step 1: Screen for behavioral change
22	
23	Screen each part of the change separately. If a part is non-behavioral, skip it. Call a part non-behavioral only when the changed code does not alter return values, thrown errors, caller-visible side effects, or observable state (including iteration order and emitted messages). Once a part meets that test, move on; do not inspect callers or tests for extra confirmation.
24	
25	Common non-behavioral examples: formatting, comments, whitespace; pure renames; trivial getters/setters and pass-throughs; type-only or compiler-enforced changes with no runtime effect; etc.
26	
27	Only outcomes produced by deterministic code are worth automatically testing; tests are useless on static source text and brittle on LLM output. Skip those parts.
28	
29	If every part is skipped, output the clean result (see Output Format).
30	
31	### Step 2: Find the behavior that changed
32	
33	Identify what behavior changed compared to the previous version: output, side effect, branch, error path, schema/event shape, config default, validation/authorization rule, external contract, etc. If the change affects more than one behavior, handle each separately.
34	
35	Treat broad-impact changes as behavioral even when no single changed line looks important: dependency, toolchain, build/config, data-file, etc.
36	
37	### Step 3: Trace where that behavior is used
38	
39	Trace the changed behavior to the places that observe it. Start with direct callers and registered entry points (routes, commands, DI), contract consumers (schemas, events, APIs, database readers), and reverse-dependency info if already available.
40	
41	Follow a path only while the changed behavior is reachable and unverified. Stop when a test at that boundary would fail, the consumer does not observe the changed behavior, or the next hop is guesswork (dynamic dispatch, reflection, outside-repo consumers, etc.). Prefer the nearest observable boundary, often one to three hops away, especially across contract, integration, or service edges. If there are more than five similar consumers, group obvious repeats and check representative paths; expand only when a consumer observes the behavior differently.
42	
43	### Step 4: Qualify the consumer, then check its test
44	
45	For each consumer, name the smallest realistic regression this consumer would observe: invert the branch, drop the default, omit the field, return the old error code, skip the integration call, etc. This is the Demonstration. If no such regression exists, drop the path; untested downstream code is not a finding.
46	
47	A `Missing-adoption gap` qualifies not by the adoption failure alone but by a supersession signal: the change gives clear evidence the new behavior is meant to replace the local one — PR intent, naming or docs, a replaced sibling site, deleted duplicate logic, or a test defining the new rule — and the local site shares the same observable contract. Without a supersession signal and a shared observable contract, it is a refactor suggestion, not a verification-gap finding. Once both hold, check whether any test for that site would flag the non-adoption; missing coverage of the non-adoption is the gap itself, not a disqualifier.
48	
49	Find and read the relevant test. Ask whether the Demonstration would make an assertion fail.
50	
51	- If yes, the behavior is verified. No finding.
52	- For a regression-style Demonstration: if no test runs the path, the test is skipped/flaky/not run normally, or the test runs the code without checking the changed result, report a `Regression gap` or `Broken-verification gap`.
53	- For a qualifying Missing-adoption case: if none of the site tests you found assert it adopts the new behavior, report a `Missing-adoption gap`.
54	
55	A test counts only if it runs normally and an assertion observes the changed output, branch, or contract. These do not count: no execution; source-text assertions that match a file's wording instead of running it; success/no-throw/snapshot-only checks; mock/log-call checks; human-only checks; tests that mock away the integration; e2e tests that pass through without checking the changed output; stale assertions or fixtures.
56	
57	For example, `expect(x ?? DEFAULT).toBe(DEFAULT)` passes when `x` is missing.
58	
59	Common patterns:
60	
61	- **Caller-path gap** — helper test covers the branch, but caller values skip it.
62	- **Contract drift** — payload/schema/event changes must be verified at the consumer.
63	- **Migration compatibility** — tests only create new-format rows or fresh schemas.
64	- **Phantom exception** — handled partial-failure path has no test.
65	- **Missing-adoption gap** — sibling site should use the new rule/helper and does not.
66	- **Removed verification** — deleted test or weakened assertion leaves behavior unpinned; removing a source-text assertion is not this, since it never counted.
67	
68	### Step 5: Confirm each finding is real
69	
70	Before writing a finding, re-open the specific tests or search results the finding relies on. Verify the Demonstration would not make any test you checked fail, or that the absence claim is backed by the symbol/import-reference search. Do not claim more than you verified; drop any finding you cannot ground.
71	
72	Explain why the test misses the bug using what the test sets up and checks.
73	
74	Do not report: compiler/type-checker-enforced cases; behavior already verified by an integration, contract, or e2e test; implementation-detail or mock-only tests; low coverage or a missing test file by itself; legacy untested code the change did not affect.
75	
76	Report genuine problems you noticed while tracing verification, even if they are not verification gaps. Put them under `Other findings` in the output. This permits reporting what you already reached, not extra hunting. A claim that code misbehaves is a defect, not a gap — it goes under `Other findings` for standard triage, however you found it.
77	
78	## OUTPUT FORMAT
79	
80	Emit each verification-gap finding as one block. No general advice, no severity or confidence. Triage trusts a gap finding as filed and does not re-verify it, so each block must stand on its own evidence.
81	
82	```markdown
83	### <one-line title naming the gap>
84	
85	- **Changed surface:** the exact behavior or contract that changed — `file:line`.
86	- **Impacted consumer or site:** named concretely with `file:line` (e.g. "the `createInvoice` mutation used by the billing dashboard at `billing/dashboard.ts:88`," not "callers of this function").
87	- **Existing test evidence:**
88	  - `Regression gap`: what the relevant test actually asserts, with `file:line`; or, if none, the symbol/import-reference searches run and their result.
89	  - `Missing-adoption gap`: tests for the impacted site, and whether any assert it adopts the new behavior.
90	  - `Broken-verification gap`: the apparent test or verification path, and why it does not count.
91	- **Missing verification:** the precise assertion or check that's absent.
92	- **Demonstration:**
93	  - `Regression gap` / `Broken-verification gap`: the concrete regression that would ship undetected, and why the tests you checked would not fail.
94	  - `Missing-adoption gap`: the case the site mishandles by not adopting the new behavior, and that none of the tests you read assert adoption.
95	- **Consequence:** the concrete thing that ships wrong — a regression the checked evidence would not catch, or a site that should use the new behavior and doesn't.
96	- **Disposition:** `patch` — name the test to add, fit to the repo's own way of verifying (don't impose a generic test pyramid) — or `defer` when the gap is real but not worth closing as part of this change, with one sentence of why.
97	```
98	
99	If you noticed genuine non-gap problems while tracing verification, append:
100	
101	```markdown
102	## Other findings
103	
104	- <description only; no severity, confidence, priority, or ranking>
105	```
106	
107	When you find no verification gaps and no other findings, output exactly this single line, not an empty response:
108	
109	`No verification gaps found.`
110	
111	## CONTENT SOURCE
112	
113	"Review content:" in the message that launched you gives the content itself or a path to read it from. Read the file when it is a path; either way that is the content under review, and this instruction file never is. If no content is supplied, or the file it points to is missing, empty, or unreadable, say exactly that and stop — never report a clean review for content you could not read.
114

9	+type: 'feature'

18	+
19	+<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">
20	+
21	+## Intent
22	+
23	+**Problem:** The shell stacks header, list and a fixed 480px map in one column at every width. There is no desktop sidebar/map split, no phone List/Map toggle, no phone details header, and sizes and spacing drift from `design/README.md` (CAP-4 full pass).
24	+
25	+**Approach:** Restyle the shell to the designed layouts. On desktop (≥ 768px), show a green header, a 400px independently scrolling sidebar and an edge-to-edge map filling the rest. On phone, show one column with a List / Map toggle (List default), and details full screen with "‹ All parks" in the green header. Then align sizes with the design and close the accessibility checklist, including Leaflet's own controls.
26	+
27	+## Boundaries & Constraints
28	+
29	+**Always:** keep the landmarks `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">` and `<section aria-label="Map of parks">`; exactly one `<h1>` per view, including loading/error; every interactive element (Leaflet zoom buttons included) shows the amber focus ring and is ≥ 44×44px; the toggle is two `<button type="button">`s with `aria-pressed`; keep 1.2–1.4 behaviour (heading focus, card focus return, marker activation by `useIsDesktop`, preview, marker refocus); no horizontal scroll at 375px; OSM attribution stays visible.
30	+
31	+**Never:** new map features (panning on select, clustering); search, filters or geolocation; editing `parks.sample.json` or `src/data/parks.js`; a component library. Layout switches with Tailwind `md:` classes. `useIsDesktop` keeps deciding behaviour only.
32	+
33	+**Decisions (agent-made, user-invisible or design-permitted):**
34	+- The header has an `aria-hidden` tree icon and the app name as a `<p>`. On desktop it reads "Parks in the New York City area" with "12 parks" on the right; on phone, "12 parks · New York City area".
35	+- On phone details the header shows only the "‹ All parks" link, and the sidebar's bordered back link is desktop-only.
36	+- Loading/error sit under an "All parks" `<h1>`.
37	+- `document.title` is "Find a Park" on the list and "{name} · Find a Park" on details.
38	+- The toggle state lives in `App` and is ignored at desktop width.
39	+- Switching to List closes any open preview.
40	+
41	+**Decisions (human):** On phone, Back from details opened via the map (marker → preview → "View park details") returns to the Map tab with focus on that park's marker. Back from details opened via the list returns to the List tab with focus on the card. The tab the details were opened from is remembered in `App` state, so browser Back works too.
42	+
43	+## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix
44	+
45	+| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
46	+|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
47	+| Phone list default | `/`, < 768px | List `aria-pressed="true"`, Map `"false"`; map pane hidden | N/A |
48	+| Phone Map tab | press Map | Map pressed; list pane hidden; map pane shown and resized to fill it (tiles not grey) | N/A |
49	+| Phone details | `/parks/:id`, < 768px | no toggle, no map; header "All parks" link; one `<h1>` | N/A |
50	+| Desktop | ≥ 768px, any route | header with count; 400px aside scrolls alone; map fills rest; no toggle | N/A |
51	+| Resize | phone Map tab → desktop → phone | both panes on desktop; map re-sizes; no stale preview | N/A |
52	+| Loading / error | fetch pending / rejects | "All parks" `<h1>` + status card | Retry works |
53	+
54	+</frozen-after-approval>
55	+

873	+/**
874	+ * Keeps Leaflet's size in step with its container. Leaflet measures once, so a container that
875	+ * was `display:none` (phone List tab) or changed size (resize, tab switch) shows grey tiles until
876	+ * `invalidateSize()` runs. Zero-size observations (pane hidden) are skipped so the view survives
877	+ * hide/show. If the map was created while hidden, the initial view is applied once it first gets a size.
878	+ */
879	+function ResizeToContainer({ fitInitialView }) {
880	+  const map = useMap()
881	+
882	+  useEffect(() => {
883	+    if (typeof ResizeObserver === 'undefined') return undefined
884	+    const container = map.getContainer()
885	+    const start = map.getSize()
886	+    let everSized = start.x > 0 && start.y > 0
887	+    const observer = new ResizeObserver((entries) => {
888	+      const rect = entries[entries.length - 1]?.contentRect
889	+      if (!rect || rect.width === 0 || rect.height === 0) return
890	+      map.invalidateSize()
891	+      if (!everSized) {
892	+        everSized = true
893	+        fitInitialView(map)
894	+      }
895	+    })
896	+    observer.observe(container)
897	+    return () => observer.disconnect()
898	+  }, [map, fitInitialView])
899	+
900	+  return null
901	+}
902	+

904	  * OpenStreetMap map of the parks that have coordinates. Each marker is a single focusable
905	  * `<button>` named "N. Park name"; the highlighted park's marker is amber and larger.
906	@@ -79,14 +110,27 @@ export default function ParkMap({ parks, selectedId, onActivate, focusId, onFocu
907	     onFocused?.()

7	+---
8	+title: 'Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied'
9	+type: 'feature'
10	+created: '2026-10-14'
11	+baseline_commit: 'ea328a0651f3c7c34484d65f6ed775576d9786e9'
12	+status: 'in-review'
13	+route: 'dispatch'
14	+review_loop_iteration: 0

16	+  - '{project-root}/_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/epic-1-context.md'
17	+---
18	+
19	+<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">
20	+
21	+## Intent
22	+
23	+**Problem:** The shell stacks header, list and a fixed 480px map in one column at every width. There is no desktop sidebar/map split, no phone List/Map toggle, no phone details header, and sizes and spacing drift from `design/README.md` (CAP-4 full pass).
24	+
25	+**Approach:** Restyle the shell to the designed layouts. On desktop (≥ 768px), show a green header, a 400px independently scrolling sidebar and an edge-to-edge map filling the rest. On phone, show one column with a List / Map toggle (List default), and details full screen with "‹ All parks" in the green header. Then align sizes with the design and close the accessibility checklist, including Leaflet's own controls.
26	+
27	+## Boundaries & Constraints
28	+
29	+**Always:** keep the landmarks `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">` and `<section aria-label="Map of parks">`; exactly one `<h1>` per view, including loading/error; every interactive element (Leaflet zoom buttons included) shows the amber focus ring and is ≥ 44×44px; the toggle is two `<button type="button">`s with `aria-pressed`; keep 1.2–1.4 behaviour (heading focus, card focus return, marker activation by `useIsDesktop`, preview, marker refocus); no horizontal scroll at 375px; OSM attribution stays visible.
30	+
31	+**Never:** new map features (panning on select, clustering); search, filters or geolocation; editing `parks.sample.json` or `src/data/parks.js`; a component library. Layout switches with Tailwind `md:` classes. `useIsDesktop` keeps deciding behaviour only.
32	+
33	+**Decisions (agent-made, user-invisible or design-permitted):**
34	+- The header has an `aria-hidden` tree icon and the app name as a `<p>`. On desktop it reads "Parks in the New York City area" with "12 parks" on the right; on phone, "12 parks · New York City area".
35	+- On phone details the header shows only the "‹ All parks" link, and the sidebar's bordered back link is desktop-only.
36	+- Loading/error sit under an "All parks" `<h1>`.
37	+- `document.title` is "Find a Park" on the list and "{name} · Find a Park" on details.
38	+- The toggle state lives in `App` and is ignored at desktop width.
39	+- Switching to List closes any open preview.
40	+
41	+**Decisions (human):** On phone, Back from details opened via the map (marker → preview → "View park details") returns to the Map tab with focus on that park's marker. Back from details opened via the list returns to the List tab with focus on the card. The tab the details were opened from is remembered in `App` state, so browser Back works too.
42	+
43	+## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix
44	+
45	+| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
46	+|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
47	+| Phone list default | `/`, < 768px | List `aria-pressed="true"`, Map `"false"`; map pane hidden | N/A |
48	+| Phone Map tab | press Map | Map pressed; list pane hidden; map pane shown and resized to fill it (tiles not grey) | N/A |
49	+| Phone details | `/parks/:id`, < 768px | no toggle, no map; header "All parks" link; one `<h1>` | N/A |
50	+| Desktop | ≥ 768px, any route | header with count; 400px aside scrolls alone; map fills rest; no toggle | N/A |
51	+| Resize | phone Map tab → desktop → phone | both panes on desktop; map re-sizes; no stale preview | N/A |
52	+| Loading / error | fetch pending / rejects | "All parks" `<h1>` + status card | Retry works |
53	+
54	+</frozen-after-approval>

873	+/**
874	+ * Keeps Leaflet's size in step with its container. Leaflet measures once, so a container that
875	+ * was `display:none` (phone List tab) or changed size (resize, tab switch) shows grey tiles until
876	+ * `invalidateSize()` runs. Zero-size observations (pane hidden) are skipped so the view survives
877	+ * hide/show. If the map was created while hidden, the initial view is applied once it first gets a size.
878	+ */
879	+function ResizeToContainer({ fitInitialView }) {
880	+  const map = useMap()
881	+
882	+  useEffect(() => {
883	+    if (typeof ResizeObserver === 'undefined') return undefined
884	+    const container = map.getContainer()
885	+    const start = map.getSize()
886	+    let everSized = start.x > 0 && start.y > 0
887	+    const observer = new ResizeObserver((entries) => {
888	+      const rect = entries[entries.length - 1]?.contentRect
889	+      if (!rect || rect.width === 0 || rect.height === 0) return
890	+      map.invalidateSize()
891	+      if (!everSized) {
892	+        everSized = true
893	+        fitInitialView(map)
894	+      }
895	+    })
896	+    observer.observe(container)
897	+    return () => observer.disconnect()
898	+  }, [map, fitInitialView])
899	+
900	+  return null
901	+}
902	+

904	  * OpenStreetMap map of the parks that have coordinates. Each marker is a single focusable
905	  * `<button>` named "N. Park name"; the highlighted park's marker is amber and larger.
906	@@ -79,14 +110,27 @@ export default function ParkMap({ parks, selectedId, onActivate, focusId, onFocu

918	+    [bounds],
919	+  )

922	+    (map) => {
923	+      if (viewProps.bounds) map.fitBounds(viewProps.bounds, viewProps.boundsOptions)
924	+      else map.setView(viewProps.center, viewProps.zoom)
925	+    },
926	+    [viewProps],

7	+---
8	+title: 'Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied'
9	+type: 'feature'
10	+created: '2026-10-14'
11	+baseline_commit: 'ea328a0651f3c7c34484d65f6ed775576d9786e9'
